#### Special notes for your reviewer:
<details>
<summary>1. ResourceSlice published by NVIDIA DRA driver</summary>

```sh
$ kubectl get resourceslice gcp-a100-test-gfn26-worker-f-gz764-gpu.nvidia.com-tt5gs \
    -o jsonpath='{.spec.sharedCounters[0]}'
gpu-0-counter-set: memory=40320Mi

$ kubectl get resourceslice gcp-a100-test-gfn26-worker-f-gz764-gpu.nvidia.com-tt5gs \
    -o jsonpath='{range .spec.devices[*]}{.name}: {.consumesCounters[0].counters.memory.value}{"\n"}{end}' \
    | grep 'mig-1g\|mig-7g'
gpu-0-mig-1g10gb-15-0: 9984Mi
gpu-0-mig-7g40gb-0-0: 40320Mi
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>2. Kueue config</summary>

```yaml
counterMappings:
- name: gpu.memory
  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
  counterName: memory
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>3. ClusterQueue with counter quota</summary>

```sh
$ kubectl get clusterqueue pd-test-cq \
    -o jsonpath='{range .spec.resourceGroups[0].flavors[0].resources[*]}{.name}: {.nominalQuota}{"\n"}{end}'
cpu: 100
memory: 200Gi
gpu.memory: 40Gi
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>4. Workload admitted with counter-based charge</summary>

```sh
$ kubectl get workload job-mig-1g-test-8be29 -n pd-test \
    -o jsonpath='{.status.admission.podSetAssignments[0].resourceUsage}'
{"cpu":"100m","gpu.memory":"9984Mi","memory":"256Mi"}
```

`1g.10gb` charges `9984Mi` and `7g.40gb` would charge `40320Mi`. Without counter-based quota, both would charge "1 GPU" via `deviceClassMappings` despite `7g.40gb` using 4x the memory.

</details>

## Pull request overview

Updates KEP-2941 documentation to describe Kueue support for DRA partitionable devices (counter-based quota), alongside the existing ResourceClaimTemplate and extended-resource integration paths.

**Changes:**
- Documented a third DRA integration approach: partitionable devices with counter-based quota derived from ResourceSlice counters.
- Expanded design details with the `DRAPartitionableDevices` feature gate and new `counterMappings` configuration concept.
- Updated KEP metadata to reference KEP-4815 and list the new feature gate.

Copilot reviewed 2 out of 2 changed files in this pull request and generated 5 comments.

| keps/2941-DRA/kep.yaml | Adds KEP-4815 link and `DRAPartitionableDevices` feature gate to KEP metadata. |

+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Risks Specific to Partitionable Devices](#risks-specific-to-partitionable-devices).
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+In K8s 1.36, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` became mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34-1.35 this validation does not exist and drivers like
+NVIDIA k8s-dra-driver-gpu v25.12 put both in one slice (`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool
+completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount` so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the K8s 1.36 layout with separate slices. On 1.34-1.35 the same
+data is in a single ResourceSlice. Only the `memory` counter is shown. The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
```

  The version-specific claim here (“On K8s 1.34-1.35 this validation does not exist”) looks inconsistent with the Kubernetes API types used by this repo (k8s.io/api v0.35.3), where ResourceSliceSpec has `+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType` and explicitly documents that only one of `devices` and `sharedCounters` can be set. Please double-check the exact Kubernetes version where this validation was introduced/enforced and adjust the wording to match reality.
  ```suggestion
  Current Kubernetes API types define `SharedCounters` and `Devices` as mutually exclusive
  in a single ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`), so drivers should publish
  them in separate slices in the same pool. Some drivers have also been observed to publish
  both in one slice (`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks
  `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`, so Kueue handles both layouts.
  
  The example below shows the layout with separate slices. In the single-slice layout, the
  same data appears in one ResourceSlice. Only the `memory` counter is shown. The driver
  also publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
  ```

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1023** by [@Copilot](https://github.com/apps/copilot-pull-request-reviewer) — 2026-04-03T03:15:24Z
```diff
@@ -800,6 +831,268 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Risks Specific to Partitionable Devices](#risks-specific-to-partitionable-devices).
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+In K8s 1.36, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` became mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34-1.35 this validation does not exist and drivers like
+NVIDIA k8s-dra-driver-gpu v25.12 put both in one slice (`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool
+completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount` so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the K8s 1.36 layout with separate slices. On 1.34-1.35 the same
+data is in a single ResourceSlice. Only the `memory` counter is shown. The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
```

  The CEL selector example doesn’t match the ResourceSlice attribute key shown above. The ResourceSlice uses an attribute key `gpu.nvidia.com/profile`, but the selector references `device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile`, which is unlikely to evaluate as intended. Please update the example to reference the correct attribute key/type so users can copy/paste a working selector.
  ```suggestion
                expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com/profile'].string == '1g.10gb'"
  ```

 ## Summary
 
 [Dynamic Resource Allocation (DRA)](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/dynamic-resource-allocation/)
 is a major effort to improve device support in Kubernetes. It changes how one can request resources in a myriad of ways.
 
-This KEP supports two approaches for DRA integration with Kueue:
+This KEP supports three approaches for DRA integration with Kueue:
 1. **ResourceClaimTemplates**: Pods explicitly reference ResourceClaimTemplates that specify device requests.
 2. **Extended Resources**: Pods request DRA devices via standard `resources.requests` (e.g., `example.com/gpu: 1`), and kube-scheduler automatically creates ResourceClaims when the DeviceClass has an `extendedResourceName` field set.
+3. **Partitionable Devices**: Counter-based quota for devices that can be dynamically
```

 ## Summary
 
 [Dynamic Resource Allocation (DRA)](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/dynamic-resource-allocation/)
 is a major effort to improve device support in Kubernetes. It changes how one can request resources in a myriad of ways.
 
-This KEP supports two approaches for DRA integration with Kueue:

 2. **Extended Resources**: Pods request DRA devices via standard `resources.requests` (e.g., `example.com/gpu: 1`), and kube-scheduler automatically creates ResourceClaims when the DeviceClass has an `extendedResourceName` field set.

 ## Summary
 
 [Dynamic Resource Allocation (DRA)](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/dynamic-resource-allocation/)
 is a major effort to improve device support in Kubernetes. It changes how one can request resources in a myriad of ways.
 
-This KEP supports two approaches for DRA integration with Kueue:
+This KEP supports three approaches for DRA integration with Kueue:
 1. **ResourceClaimTemplates**: Pods explicitly reference ResourceClaimTemplates that specify device requests.
 2. **Extended Resources**: Pods request DRA devices via standard `resources.requests` (e.g., `example.com/gpu: 1`), and kube-scheduler automatically creates ResourceClaims when the DeviceClass has an `extendedResourceName` field set.
+3. **Partitionable Devices**: Counter-based quota for devices that can be dynamically
```

  Sure, I can add a short blurb about that. So the consumable capacity devices feature will require a separate effort as Kueue would need to read the cost from the claim's capacity request, which is a different data path.
  NVIDIA’s DRA driver doesn’t support consumable capacity yet, even though the API is moving to beta in Kubernetes 1.36. The underlying GPU mechanisms (MPS and time-slicing) still have key limitations.
  MPS lacks isolation i.e. one crashing CUDA process can affect all workloads on the GPU. Time-slicing has no memory isolation, which likely explains why these haven't integrated into their DRA driver yet.

### Review by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) (commented) — 2026-04-08T00:57:38Z

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1004** by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) — 2026-04-08T00:57:38Z
```diff
@@ -800,6 +850,268 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Risks Specific to Partitionable Devices](#risks-specific-to-partitionable-devices).
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. This is separate from `deviceClassMappings` following the path separation principle.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterMapping maps a DRA driver counter to a Kueue quota resource for
+// partitionable device support.
+type CounterMapping struct {
+    // Name is the Kueue resource name used in ClusterQueue coveredResources
+    // and Workload resourceUsage.
+    // Must follow the same naming rules as DeviceClassMapping.Name.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Name corev1.ResourceName `json:"name"`
+
+    // Driver is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the `spec.driver` field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Driver string `json:"driver"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set) so requiring one mapping per GPU would be impractical.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+}
+```
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  counterMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    driver: gpu.nvidia.com
```

  do you mean `mig.nvidia.com` here?

```diff
@@ -800,6 +850,288 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Risks Specific to Partitionable Devices](#risks-specific-to-partitionable-devices).
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. Each counter mapping binds a DeviceClass to a counter-based quota resource. A
+DeviceClass can appear in either `deviceClassMappings` or `counterMappings` but not both.
+This is validated at config load time.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterMapping maps a DRA driver counter to a Kueue quota resource for
+// partitionable device support.
+type CounterMapping struct {
+    // Name is the Kueue resource name used in ClusterQueue coveredResources
+    // and Workload resourceUsage.
+    // Must follow the same naming rules as DeviceClassMapping.Name.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Name corev1.ResourceName `json:"name"`
+
+    // DeviceClassNames lists the DeviceClasses that use counter-based quota.
+    // A DeviceClass can appear in either deviceClassMappings or counterMappings
+    // but not both. This is validated at config load time.
+    DeviceClassNames []corev1.ResourceName `json:"deviceClassNames"`
+
+    // Driver is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Driver string `json:"driver"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set) so requiring one mapping per GPU would be impractical.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+}
+```
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: whole-gpu
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu.nvidia.com]
+
+  counterMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+    counterName: memory
+```
+
+`gpu.nvidia.com` uses device counting (deviceClassMappings). `mig.nvidia.com` uses
+counter-based quota (counterMappings). A DeviceClass cannot appear in both. This is
+enforced at config load time.
+
+Counter names are driver-specific (NVIDIA uses `memory`, another vendor may use `gpu-mem`),
+so `counterMappings` translates them to Kueue quota resource names. Not all counters are
+quota-relevant. NVIDIA MIG devices have 15+ counter dimensions. The admin chooses which
+counters to track for quota. The scheduler handles the rest.
+
+ClusterQueue uses counter resources like any other resource:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: mig-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-mig-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "320Gi"
+```
+
+#### Processing Flow
+
+Partitionable devices reuses the upstream `dracel` compiler from
+[#7298](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/7298) but performs its own
+ResourceSlice listing with pool-aware processing, since the CEL validation path does not
+return matched device objects or do pool grouping:
+
+1. Kueue detects that the RCT's DeviceClass is in `counterMappings`
+2. Lists ResourceSlices for the configured driver, groups them by pool and checks completeness
+3. Compiles and evaluates CEL selectors against devices in complete pools
+4. For matched devices, reads `consumesCounters` entries
+5. Resolves the quota resource name from the matching `counterMappings` entry
+6. Uses the maximum consumption across matched devices as the per-device counter charge
+7. For requests with `count > 1`, multiplies per-device consumption by count
+
+Counter resources are injected through the existing `WithPreprocessedDRAResources` path.
+
+In Alpha, partitionable devices performs its own ResourceSlice listing independently from
+the CEL validation path because the two have different requirements: CEL validation only
+needs a match count, while counter processing needs matched device objects and pool-aware
+grouping. In Beta, these can be consolidated into a shared ResourceSlice listing layer.
+
+When no devices match or ResourceSlice data is unavailable, the workload is marked
+inadmissible. Workloads are never admitted with zero counter charge when `counterMappings`
+are configured. Kueue only considers ResourceSlices from the pool with the highest
+generation. If the slice count is less than `resourceSliceCount`, the pool is incomplete
+and the workload is marked inadmissible.
+
+When CEL selectors match devices with different profiles (e.g., both 1g.10gb and 7g.80gb),
+Kueue charges the maximum consumption. Users should write specific CEL selectors that
+narrow to a single profile for accurate quota.
```

  When count > 1, the per-device charge (MAX) is multiplied by count. This can overcharge when matched devices have different counter values — for example, if a CEL selector matches devices with memory >= 80Gi  and 3 devices match (80Gi, 90Gi, 100Gi) with count=2, the charge is 100Gi × 2 = 200Gi, whereas the actual worst-case allocation is 100Gi + 90Gi = 190Gi. Should we add this as an explicit alpha limitation?

+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Risks Specific to Partitionable Devices](#risks-specific-to-partitionable-devices).
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. Each counter mapping binds a DeviceClass to a counter-based quota resource. A
+DeviceClass can appear in either `deviceClassMappings` or `counterMappings` but not both.
+This is validated at config load time.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterMapping maps a DRA driver counter to a Kueue quota resource for
+// partitionable device support.
+type CounterMapping struct {
+    // Name is the Kueue resource name used in ClusterQueue coveredResources
+    // and Workload resourceUsage.
+    // Must follow the same naming rules as DeviceClassMapping.Name.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Name corev1.ResourceName `json:"name"`
+
+    // DeviceClassNames lists the DeviceClasses that use counter-based quota.
+    // A DeviceClass can appear in either deviceClassMappings or counterMappings
+    // but not both. This is validated at config load time.
+    DeviceClassNames []corev1.ResourceName `json:"deviceClassNames"`
+
+    // Driver is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Driver string `json:"driver"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set) so requiring one mapping per GPU would be impractical.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+}
+```
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: whole-gpu
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu.nvidia.com]
+
+  counterMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+    counterName: memory
+```
+
+`gpu.nvidia.com` uses device counting (deviceClassMappings). `mig.nvidia.com` uses
+counter-based quota (counterMappings). A DeviceClass cannot appear in both. This is
+enforced at config load time.
+
+Counter names are driver-specific (NVIDIA uses `memory`, another vendor may use `gpu-mem`),
+so `counterMappings` translates them to Kueue quota resource names. Not all counters are
+quota-relevant. NVIDIA MIG devices have 15+ counter dimensions. The admin chooses which
+counters to track for quota. The scheduler handles the rest.
+
+ClusterQueue uses counter resources like any other resource:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: mig-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-mig-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "320Gi"
+```
+
+#### Processing Flow
+
+Partitionable devices reuses the upstream `dracel` compiler from
+[#7298](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/7298) but performs its own
+ResourceSlice listing with pool-aware processing, since the CEL validation path does not
+return matched device objects or do pool grouping:
+
+1. Kueue detects that the RCT's DeviceClass is in `counterMappings`
+2. Lists ResourceSlices for the configured driver, groups them by pool and checks completeness
+3. Compiles and evaluates CEL selectors against devices in complete pools
+4. For matched devices, reads `consumesCounters` entries
+5. Resolves the quota resource name from the matching `counterMappings` entry
+6. Uses the maximum consumption across matched devices as the per-device counter charge
+7. For requests with `count > 1`, multiplies per-device consumption by count
+
+Counter resources are injected through the existing `WithPreprocessedDRAResources` path.
+
+In Alpha, partitionable devices performs its own ResourceSlice listing independently from
+the CEL validation path because the two have different requirements: CEL validation only
+needs a match count, while counter processing needs matched device objects and pool-aware
+grouping. In Beta, these can be consolidated into a shared ResourceSlice listing layer.
+
+When no devices match or ResourceSlice data is unavailable, the workload is marked
+inadmissible. Workloads are never admitted with zero counter charge when `counterMappings`
+are configured. Kueue only considers ResourceSlices from the pool with the highest
+generation. If the slice count is less than `resourceSliceCount`, the pool is incomplete
+and the workload is marked inadmissible.
+
+When CEL selectors match devices with different profiles (e.g., both 1g.10gb and 7g.80gb),
+Kueue charges the maximum consumption. Users should write specific CEL selectors that
+narrow to a single profile for accurate quota.
```

  I added explicit note for the count > 1 case. The alpha behavior is intentionally conservative: max * count rather than top-N summation. In practice, users write profile-specific selectors so all matched devices have the same counter values and max * count equals actual consumption.

+

+```yaml

+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:

+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. Each counter mapping binds a DeviceClass to a counter-based quota resource. A
+DeviceClass can appear in either `deviceClassMappings` or `counterMappings` but not both.
+This is validated at config load time.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
```

  While this API is a good start for counter-based quota, I think it falls short for the case where an admin wants a single physical GPU pool to serve both whole-device and partitioned-device requests.
  
  With the proposed API, the ClusterQueue ends up with two independent quota dimensions, one for whole devices and one for partitioned devices:
  ```
  apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
  kind: ClusterQueue
  metadata:
    name: mig-queue
  spec:
    resourceGroups:
    - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory", "whole-gpus"]
      flavors:
      - name: partitionable-device
        resources:
        - name: gpu.memory
          nominalQuota: "320Gi"
      - name: whole-device
         resources:
         - name: whole-gups
            nominalQuota: "10"
  ```
  
  There is no mechanism to express that these quotas are backed by the same physical devices, or that admitting a whole-GPU workload should reduce the available partitionable-device counter budget, and vice versa.
  
  That seems to bring us back toward the device-plugin model, where the admin decides ahead of time how much capacity is exposed as “whole GPUs” versus “MIG/time-sliced/MPS-style” capacity. The DRA model should let the user request drive the shape of the allocation through the ResourceClaim, with Kueue accounting against the shared underlying device capacity.

+

+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
```

  Could we explore an alternative API shape where counters are attached to the `deviceClassMappings` entry instead of being exposed as a separate top-level quota resource?
  
  For example:
  ```
  resources:
    deviceClassMappings:
    - name: gpus
      deviceClassNames:
      - gpu.example.com
      - partitionable-device-class.example.com
      counters:
      - driverName: gpu.example.com
        name: memory
  ---
  apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
  kind: ClusterQueue
  metadata:
    name: gpu-queue
  spec:
    resourceGroups:
    - coveredResources: ["gpus"]
      flavors:
      - name: whole-along-with-partitions-gpus
        resources:
        - name: gpus
           nominalQuota: "10"
  ```
  
  With this shape, the cluster admin only sets quota on the parent resource, gpus. Kueue can then derive the available counter budget for each configured counter dimension from ResourceSlices, for example 10 * sharedCounters["memory"] for the selected flavor/pool.
  
  A whole-device allocation would consume one unit of gpus and the full relevant counter capacity for the allocated device. A partitioned allocation would consume from the same gpus counter budget using the device’s consumesCounters values. This keeps whole-device and partitioned-device requests tied to the same underlying physical capacity, while still letting the user’s ResourceClaim determine the actual device shape.

+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. Each counter mapping binds a DeviceClass to a counter-based quota resource. A
+DeviceClass can appear in either `deviceClassMappings` or `counterMappings` but not both.
+This is validated at config load time.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
```

  You're right, the separate struct creates two disconnected quota pools for the same hardware. I will rework it, so counters can be a field on `deviceClassMappings`. Both whole-GPU and MIG go in one entry, one pool.

### Review by [@sohankunkerkar](https://github.com/sohankunkerkar) (commented) — 2026-05-11T20:46:22Z

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1089** by [@sohankunkerkar](https://github.com/sohankunkerkar) — 2026-05-11T20:46:22Z
```diff
@@ -873,6 +922,293 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Counter Lifecycle Scenarios](#counter-lifecycle-scenarios) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. Each counter mapping binds a DeviceClass to a counter-based quota resource. A
+DeviceClass can appear in either `deviceClassMappings` or `counterMappings` but not both.
+This is validated at config load time.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterMapping maps a DRA driver counter to a Kueue quota resource for
+// partitionable device support.
+type CounterMapping struct {
+    // Name is the Kueue resource name used in ClusterQueue coveredResources
+    // and Workload resourceUsage.
+    // Must follow the same naming rules as DeviceClassMapping.Name.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Name corev1.ResourceName `json:"name"`
+
+    // DeviceClassNames lists the DeviceClasses that use counter-based quota.
+    // A DeviceClass can appear in either deviceClassMappings or counterMappings
+    // but not both. This is validated at config load time.
+    DeviceClassNames []corev1.ResourceName `json:"deviceClassNames"`
+
+    // Driver is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Driver string `json:"driver"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set) so requiring one mapping per GPU would be impractical.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+}
+```
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
```

  Yes, it makes sense to think from the admin mental model here, so this approach fits well. I kept your idea of putting counters inside `deviceClassMappings`, but used counter units directly as the quota value.                                                                                                                                                                          
  
  The issue with a device-count model plus a derived counter budget is that Kueue would need to track two coupled dimensions (device count and counter budget), handle fractional devices for MIG workloads, and compute budgets dynamically from ResourceSlice data , none of which exist in Kueue today.  For example, say an admin sets nominalQuota: "10" (devices) and Kueue derives a counter budget from sharedCounters. A whole-GPU workload is easy as  it consumes 1 device. But a MIG workload consumes a counter value (the driver publishes 9856Mi for 1g.10gb). Converting that back to a device fraction requires knowing the GPU's total capacity, and that ratio changes across GPU models and counter dimensions. There's no clean single number, so Kueue would need to track both dimensions. Using counter units keeps things simple since they behave like normal resources such as CPU or memory. The admin UX tradeoff is fair, but admins already need to know GPU specs while setting up flavors, so this doesn't feel like a huge additional burden.

  > The issue with a device-count model plus a derived counter budget is that Kueue would need to track two coupled dimensions (device count and counter budget), handle fractional devices for MIG workloads, and compute budgets dynamically from ResourceSlice data , none of which exist in Kueue today. For example, say an admin sets nominalQuota: "10" (devices) and Kueue derives a counter budget from sharedCounters. A whole-GPU workload is easy as it consumes 1 device. But a MIG workload consumes a counter value (the driver publishes 9856Mi for 1g.10gb). 
  
  I don’t think we need to convert counter usage back into fractional GPUs. That is not the model I am proposing.
  
  The model I have in mind is that a physical GPU has two coupled accounting dimensions:
  
  1. whole-device availability: can this physical GPU still satisfy a whole-GPU request?
  2. partitionable counter usage: how much of each configured counter, such as memory, has been consumed?
  
  Once Kueue admits a partitioned allocation on a physical GPU, that GPU should no longer be counted as available for whole-GPU admission. Separately, Kueue should track the configured counter vectors, such as memory, so additional partitioned allocations can still be admitted against the remaining counter capacity on that same physical GPU.
  
  
  For example, with:
  
  ```yaml
  resources:
    deviceClassMappings:
    - name: gpus
      deviceClassNames:
      - gpu.example.com
      - mig.nvidia.com
      counters:
      - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
        counterName: memory
  ```
  
  If an admin configures quota in whole-device units:
  
  ```yaml
  apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
  kind: ClusterQueue
  ..
  ..
        resources:
        - name: gpus
          nominalQuota: "10"
  ```
  
  then the counter dimension should become active only when Kueue admits a partitioned allocation. At that point Kueue can use ResourceSlice data for the actual selected/opened GPU:
  - mark one physical GPU as unavailable for whole-GPU admission
  - add that GPU’s actual sharedCounters.memory value to the gpus.counters.memory budget
  - charge the claim’s actual consumesCounters.memory value against that budget
  
  Conceptually, after admitting a 1g.10gb claim on a GPU whose ResourceSlice reports sharedCounters.memory = 188928Mi and whose selected device reports consumesCounters.memory = 9984Mi, the accounting could look like:
  
  ```
  status:
    flavorsUsage:
    - name: gpu-pool
      resources:
      - name: gpus
        nominalQuota: "10"
        used: "1" # one physical GPU is no longer available as a whole GPU
      - name: gpus.counters.memory
        nominalQuota: "188928Mi" # actual opened GPU counter capacity
        used: "9984Mi"           # actual partition consumption
  ```
  
  with the above, I dont think the `defaultConsumption` field is needed.

  Thanks for the detailed model, I think I understand it now. What you're describing is the precise physical model: when a MIG allocation lands on a GPU, that GPU’s specific counter capacity gets reduced, and future allocations must fit within the remaining capacity of that same physical GPU.
  
  The challenge is that Kueue quota tracking today is purely aggregate `(Flavor, Resource) -> Quantity`. Everything in the quota path works on totals, not on which physical GPU contributed the capacity. Supporting per-GPU counter budgets would require a new dimension in the quota model.
  
  What I'm proposing for Alpha is an aggregate approximation: admins set `nominalQuota` in counter units, and MIG workloads charge their `consumesCounters` values against that pool. This does not enforce per-GPU limits directly, but the scheduler already handles placement while Kueue tracks overall consumption using the existing borrowing/preemption model unchanged.
  
  This is similar to how Kueue already treats non-DRA resources today: quota is an approximation of physical capacity, and `waitForPodsReady` is the safety net when admission and scheduling diverge. I think this aggregate model is reasonable for Alpha, while proper per-GPU tracking feels more aligned with the TAS + DRA integration work already planned for graduation.

+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Counter Lifecycle Scenarios](#counter-lifecycle-scenarios) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. Each counter mapping binds a DeviceClass to a counter-based quota resource. A
+DeviceClass can appear in either `deviceClassMappings` or `counterMappings` but not both.
+This is validated at config load time.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterMapping maps a DRA driver counter to a Kueue quota resource for
+// partitionable device support.
+type CounterMapping struct {
+    // Name is the Kueue resource name used in ClusterQueue coveredResources
+    // and Workload resourceUsage.
+    // Must follow the same naming rules as DeviceClassMapping.Name.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Name corev1.ResourceName `json:"name"`
+
+    // DeviceClassNames lists the DeviceClasses that use counter-based quota.
+    // A DeviceClass can appear in either deviceClassMappings or counterMappings
+    // but not both. This is validated at config load time.
+    DeviceClassNames []corev1.ResourceName `json:"deviceClassNames"`
+
+    // Driver is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Driver string `json:"driver"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set) so requiring one mapping per GPU would be impractical.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+}
+```
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
```

  > Thanks for the detailed model, I think I understand it now. What you're describing is the precise physical model: when a MIG allocation lands on a GPU, that GPU’s specific counter capacity gets reduced, and future allocations must fit within the remaining capacity of that same physical GPU.
  
  I am not asking Kueue to track the exact GPU that kube-scheduler will later allocate. I agree that exact per-GPU placement/reservation is a larger problem and is out of scope for this KEP.
  
  What I am asking is whether Kueue can derive the whole-device counter charge from ResourceSlices instead of requiring the admin to hard-code it via `defaultConsumption`.
  
  For example, if the admin configures parent device quota:
  
  ```yaml
  resources:
    deviceClassMappings:
    - name: gpus
      deviceClassNames:
      - gpu.example.com
      - mig.nvidia.com
      counters:
      - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
        counterName: memory
        selector:
          cel:
            expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com/product'].string == 'A100'"
  ```
  
  then Kueue could use ResourceSlice data matching the driver/counter/selector to derive the effective whole-device counter consumption.

  ah, I see what you are saying. That's a cleaner approach. Using a CEL selector on the counter config to derive the whole-device charge from `ResourceSlices` instead of hard-coding defaultConsumption. The selector solves the `which pool's sharedCounters?` question that made auto-derivation hard without it.
  
  For Alpha, would it be acceptable to keep `defaultConsumption` as the simpler path and add the selector-based derivation as a follow-up? The CEL selector on the counter config adds another compilation + evaluation path, and I'd like to get the core counter-based quota working first. The selector approach is the right direction for removing the admin burden. I just want to stage the complexity

  > For Alpha, would it be acceptable to keep defaultConsumption as the simpler path and add the selector-based derivation as a follow-up? The CEL selector on the counter config adds another compilation + evaluation path, and I'd like to get the core counter-based quota working first. The selector approach is the right direction for removing the admin burden. I just want to stage the complexity
  
  I would prefer not to make `defaultConsumption` the primary Alpha API if we agree selector-based derivation is the right direction.
  
  A few reasons:
  
  1. This is static Kueue configuration, so CEL compilation can happen at startup/config load time. The compilation cost should not be on the admission hot path.
  2. Kueue already has CEL evaluation machinery from https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9742, so this would reuse the same direction.
  3. For Alpha, the selector can be intentionally constrained. For example, we could only allow matching ResourceSlice/device attributes, and even start with a simple equality match on one attribute. That is enough for the main use case: letting admins identify a homogeneous device shape, such as product name.
  
  The key concept of the selector here is to give admins the ability to define devices with similar shape, other ideas that solve the same issue but avoid CEL compilation+evaluation could also be explored.

+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml

+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames, ExtendedResourceNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Mutually exclusive with ExtendedResourceNames on the same mapping entry.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // DefaultConsumption is the counter value charged for devices that do not
+    // have consumesCounters (e.g., whole GPUs that are not partitioned). When a
+    // matched device has consumesCounters, the actual value is used instead.
+    // When omitted, devices without consumesCounters are marked inadmissible.
+    // Set this when whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses share one quota pool.
+    // +optional
+    DefaultConsumption *resource.Quantity `json:"defaultConsumption,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup where `defaultConsumption` provides the charge for
+whole-GPU devices that do not have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      defaultConsumption: "40Gi"
+```
+
+When a matched device has `consumesCounters`, Kueue uses the actual value (e.g., 10Gi
+for 1g.10gb MIG). When a matched device has no `consumesCounters`, Kueue charges
+`defaultConsumption` if configured, otherwise the workload is marked inadmissible.
+
+When `counters` is absent on a mapping, the mapping behaves exactly as today (device-count
+quota). This is backward compatible and covers non-GPU DRA devices where device count is
+the appropriate unit.
+
+Counter names are driver-specific (NVIDIA uses `memory`, another vendor may use `gpu-mem`),
+so `counters` translates them to the parent mapping's Kueue quota resource name. Not all
+counters are quota-relevant. NVIDIA MIG devices have 15+ counter dimensions. The admin
+chooses which counters to track for quota. The scheduler handles the rest.
+
+ClusterQueue uses counter resources like any other resource:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "400Gi"   # 10 GPUs x 40Gi, in counter units
+```
+
+#### Processing Flow
+
+Partitionable devices reuses the upstream `dracel` compiler from
+[#7298](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/7298) but performs its own
+ResourceSlice listing with pool-aware processing, since the CEL validation path does not
+return matched device objects or do pool grouping:
+
+1. Kueue looks up the RCT's DeviceClass in `deviceClassMappings`
+2. If the mapping has `counters` configured, enters the counter-based path
+3. Lists ResourceSlices for the configured driver, groups them by pool and checks completeness
+4. Compiles DeviceClass selectors (pre-filter) and RCT request selectors using the
+   `dracel` compiler, evaluates both against devices in complete pools to produce
+   matched devices
+5. For each matched device, resolves the counter charge:
+   - Device has `consumesCounters` containing the configured `counterName`: uses the
+     actual value (e.g., 10Gi for a 1g.10gb MIG profile)
+   - Device has `consumesCounters` but `counterName` is not found: workload is
+     marked inadmissible
+   - Device has no `consumesCounters` and `defaultConsumption` is set: uses
+     `defaultConsumption` (e.g., 40Gi for a whole GPU)
+   - Device has no `consumesCounters` and `defaultConsumption` is not set: workload is
+     marked inadmissible
+6. Uses the maximum consumption across matched devices as the per-device counter charge
+7. For requests with `count > 1`, multiplies per-device consumption by count
+
+Counter resources are injected through the existing `WithPreprocessedDRAResources` path.
+
+In Alpha, partitionable devices performs its own ResourceSlice listing independently from
+the CEL validation path because the two have different requirements: CEL validation only
+needs a match count, while counter processing needs matched device objects and pool-aware
+grouping. In Beta, these can be consolidated into a shared ResourceSlice listing layer.
+
+When no devices match or ResourceSlice data is unavailable, the workload is marked
+inadmissible. Workloads are never admitted with zero counter charge when `counters` is
+configured. Kueue only considers ResourceSlices from the pool with the highest
+generation. If the slice count is less than `resourceSliceCount`, the pool is incomplete
+and the workload is marked inadmissible.
+
+When CEL selectors match devices with different profiles (e.g., both 1g.10gb and 7g.80gb),
+Kueue charges the maximum consumption. Users should write specific CEL selectors that
+narrow to a single profile for accurate quota. For `count > 1`, the per-device maximum
+is multiplied by count, which may overcharge when matched devices have different counter
+values (e.g., 100Gi x 2 = 200Gi vs actual worst-case 100Gi + 90Gi = 190Gi). This is an
+accepted Alpha limitation since profile-specific selectors produce identical values across
+matched devices.
+
+The Kubernetes API limits each device to 2 `consumesCounters` entries
+(`ResourceSliceMaxDeviceCounterConsumptionsPerDevice`), each referencing a different counter
+set. In Alpha, only single-node partitionable devices are supported where each device
+consumes from a single local counter set. Multi-counter-set semantics (e.g., a device
+consuming from both a local GPU pool and a shared NVLink fabric pool) will be addressed
+with multi-host support in future work.
+
+#### Path Interactions
+
+Since counters are part of `deviceClassMappings`, there is no cross-struct exclusivity
+to enforce. A DeviceClass appears in exactly one `deviceClassMappings` entry (existing
+validation prevents duplicates).
+
+Routing between counter-based and device-count quota is determined by config: the
+presence of `counters` on the mapping entry selects the path. Within the counter path,
+the charge per device is determined by data: `consumesCounters` on the matched device
+selects between the actual counter value and `defaultConsumption`.
+
+- `counters` and `extendedResourceNames` are mutually exclusive on the same mapping
+  entry. This is validated at config load time.
+- Using `counters` and `extendedResourceName` (the DeviceClass API field) for the same
+  DeviceClass is not supported in Alpha. This combination produces ambiguous quota
+  semantics depending on whether the workload arrives via extended resources or
+  ResourceClaimTemplates. Runtime validation will be evaluated for Beta.
```

  Maybe we can consider supporting counters alongside extendedResourceName instead of treating them as mutually exclusive. When an extended resource request (e.g., nvidia.com/gpu: 1) resolves to a DeviceClass whose mapping has counters configured, the extended resources path can detect this and charge the derived counter value instead of device count. If the mapping has no counters, the extended resource is charged as device count — same as today.                                                                                                                                                                                  
     
  This way both paths -  nvidia.com/gpu: 1 via extended resources and a MIG RCT for mig.nvidia.com would both charge against gpu.memory in counter units. WDYT?

### Review by [@sohankunkerkar](https://github.com/sohankunkerkar) (commented) — 2026-05-13T03:41:36Z

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1089** by [@sohankunkerkar](https://github.com/sohankunkerkar) — 2026-05-13T03:41:35Z
```diff
@@ -873,6 +922,293 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Counter Lifecycle Scenarios](#counter-lifecycle-scenarios) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. Each counter mapping binds a DeviceClass to a counter-based quota resource. A
+DeviceClass can appear in either `deviceClassMappings` or `counterMappings` but not both.
+This is validated at config load time.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterMapping maps a DRA driver counter to a Kueue quota resource for
+// partitionable device support.
+type CounterMapping struct {
+    // Name is the Kueue resource name used in ClusterQueue coveredResources
+    // and Workload resourceUsage.
+    // Must follow the same naming rules as DeviceClassMapping.Name.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Name corev1.ResourceName `json:"name"`
+
+    // DeviceClassNames lists the DeviceClasses that use counter-based quota.
+    // A DeviceClass can appear in either deviceClassMappings or counterMappings
+    // but not both. This is validated at config load time.
+    DeviceClassNames []corev1.ResourceName `json:"deviceClassNames"`
+
+    // Driver is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Driver string `json:"driver"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set) so requiring one mapping per GPU would be impractical.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+}
+```
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
```

  I would like to provide some findings here. I looked at the NVIDIA DRA driver code to understand the actual need. In partitionable mode (DynamicMIG), the driver publishes consumesCounters on both whole GPUs and MIG devices and whole GPUs consume the full counter set. So the primary path (consumesCounters → actual value) covers everything without needing deviceSelector.
  
  That said, this is driver-specific behavior. The K8s API allows devices without consumesCounters in pools that have sharedCounters, so other drivers may not follow this pattern. Including deviceSelector as a driver-agnostic fallback for Alpha makes sense. I will update the KEP to frame it as a fallback for drivers that don't publish consumesCounters on non-partitioned devices, not as the primary path.
