+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Counter Lifecycle Scenarios](#counter-lifecycle-scenarios) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames, ExtendedResourceNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Mutually exclusive with ExtendedResourceNames on the same mapping entry.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // DefaultConsumption is the counter value charged for devices that do not
+    // have consumesCounters (e.g., whole GPUs that are not partitioned). When a
+    // matched device has consumesCounters, the actual value is used instead.
+    // When omitted, devices without consumesCounters are marked inadmissible.
+    // Set this when whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses share one quota pool.
+    // +optional
+    DefaultConsumption *resource.Quantity `json:"defaultConsumption,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup where `defaultConsumption` provides the charge for
+whole-GPU devices that do not have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      defaultConsumption: "40Gi"
+```
+
+When a matched device has `consumesCounters`, Kueue uses the actual value (e.g., 10Gi
+for 1g.10gb MIG). When a matched device has no `consumesCounters`, Kueue charges
+`defaultConsumption` if configured, otherwise the workload is marked inadmissible.
+
+When `counters` is absent on a mapping, the mapping behaves exactly as today (device-count
+quota). This is backward compatible and covers non-GPU DRA devices where device count is
+the appropriate unit.
+
+Counter names are driver-specific (NVIDIA uses `memory`, another vendor may use `gpu-mem`),
+so `counters` translates them to the parent mapping's Kueue quota resource name. Not all
+counters are quota-relevant. NVIDIA MIG devices have 15+ counter dimensions. The admin
+chooses which counters to track for quota. The scheduler handles the rest.
+
+ClusterQueue uses counter resources like any other resource:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "400Gi"   # 10 GPUs x 40Gi, in counter units
+```
+
+#### Processing Flow
+
+Partitionable devices reuses the upstream `dracel` compiler from
+[#7298](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/7298) but performs its own
+ResourceSlice listing with pool-aware processing, since the CEL validation path does not
+return matched device objects or do pool grouping:
+
+1. Kueue looks up the RCT's DeviceClass in `deviceClassMappings`
+2. If the mapping has `counters` configured, enters the counter-based path
+3. Lists ResourceSlices for the configured driver, groups them by pool and checks completeness
+4. Compiles DeviceClass selectors (pre-filter) and RCT request selectors using the
+   `dracel` compiler, evaluates both against devices in complete pools to produce
+   matched devices
+5. For each matched device, resolves the counter charge:
+   - Device has `consumesCounters` containing the configured `counterName`: uses the
+     actual value (e.g., 10Gi for a 1g.10gb MIG profile)
+   - Device has `consumesCounters` but `counterName` is not found: workload is
+     marked inadmissible
+   - Device has no `consumesCounters` and `defaultConsumption` is set: uses
+     `defaultConsumption` (e.g., 40Gi for a whole GPU)
+   - Device has no `consumesCounters` and `defaultConsumption` is not set: workload is
+     marked inadmissible
+6. Uses the maximum consumption across matched devices as the per-device counter charge
+7. For requests with `count > 1`, multiplies per-device consumption by count
+
+Counter resources are injected through the existing `WithPreprocessedDRAResources` path.
+
+In Alpha, partitionable devices performs its own ResourceSlice listing independently from
+the CEL validation path because the two have different requirements: CEL validation only
+needs a match count, while counter processing needs matched device objects and pool-aware
+grouping. In Beta, these can be consolidated into a shared ResourceSlice listing layer.
+
+When no devices match or ResourceSlice data is unavailable, the workload is marked
+inadmissible. Workloads are never admitted with zero counter charge when `counters` is
+configured. Kueue only considers ResourceSlices from the pool with the highest
+generation. If the slice count is less than `resourceSliceCount`, the pool is incomplete
+and the workload is marked inadmissible.
+
+When CEL selectors match devices with different profiles (e.g., both 1g.10gb and 7g.80gb),
+Kueue charges the maximum consumption. Users should write specific CEL selectors that
+narrow to a single profile for accurate quota. For `count > 1`, the per-device maximum
+is multiplied by count, which may overcharge when matched devices have different counter
+values (e.g., 100Gi x 2 = 200Gi vs actual worst-case 100Gi + 90Gi = 190Gi). This is an
+accepted Alpha limitation since profile-specific selectors produce identical values across
+matched devices.
+
+The Kubernetes API limits each device to 2 `consumesCounters` entries
+(`ResourceSliceMaxDeviceCounterConsumptionsPerDevice`), each referencing a different counter
+set. In Alpha, only single-node partitionable devices are supported where each device
+consumes from a single local counter set. Multi-counter-set semantics (e.g., a device
+consuming from both a local GPU pool and a shared NVLink fabric pool) will be addressed
+with multi-host support in future work.
+
+#### Path Interactions
+
+Since counters are part of `deviceClassMappings`, there is no cross-struct exclusivity
+to enforce. A DeviceClass appears in exactly one `deviceClassMappings` entry (existing
+validation prevents duplicates).
+
+Routing between counter-based and device-count quota is determined by config: the
+presence of `counters` on the mapping entry selects the path. Within the counter path,
+the charge per device is determined by data: `consumesCounters` on the matched device
+selects between the actual counter value and `defaultConsumption`.
+
+- `counters` and `extendedResourceNames` are mutually exclusive on the same mapping
+  entry. This is validated at config load time.
+- Using `counters` and `extendedResourceName` (the DeviceClass API field) for the same
+  DeviceClass is not supported in Alpha. This combination produces ambiguous quota
+  semantics depending on whether the workload arrives via extended resources or
+  ResourceClaimTemplates. Runtime validation will be evaluated for Beta.
```

  ~Yup, agreed! will update the KEP to reflect that.~
  
  Good point about unified charging. The use case you're describing-> whole GPU + MIG from the same counter quota pool is valid. The issue is that extended resources only carry a quantity (nvidia.com/gpu: 1) with no profile-level information. The K8s scheduler auto-creates a ResourceClaim with just DeviceClassName + count, no CEL selectors or capacity requests. So Kueue can't determine the accurate counter charge. It would always be the max across all devices matching the DeviceClass. 
  
  For workloads that need both a whole GPU and a MIG slice from the same counter pool, both should use RCT with CEL selectors. The whole-GPU RCT matches devices with consumesCounters: {memory: 80Gi}, the MIG RCT matches with consumesCounters: {memory: 9856Mi}. Both charge against gpu.memory accurately. Extended resources are better suited for device-count quota where profile specificity isn't needed.

### Review by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) (commented) — 2026-05-13T14:38:28Z

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1089** by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) — 2026-05-13T14:38:27Z
```diff
@@ -873,6 +922,293 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Counter Lifecycle Scenarios](#counter-lifecycle-scenarios) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. Each counter mapping binds a DeviceClass to a counter-based quota resource. A
+DeviceClass can appear in either `deviceClassMappings` or `counterMappings` but not both.
+This is validated at config load time.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterMapping maps a DRA driver counter to a Kueue quota resource for
+// partitionable device support.
+type CounterMapping struct {
+    // Name is the Kueue resource name used in ClusterQueue coveredResources
+    // and Workload resourceUsage.
+    // Must follow the same naming rules as DeviceClassMapping.Name.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Name corev1.ResourceName `json:"name"`
+
+    // DeviceClassNames lists the DeviceClasses that use counter-based quota.
+    // A DeviceClass can appear in either deviceClassMappings or counterMappings
+    // but not both. This is validated at config load time.
+    DeviceClassNames []corev1.ResourceName `json:"deviceClassNames"`
+
+    // Driver is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    Driver string `json:"driver"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set) so requiring one mapping per GPU would be impractical.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+}
+```
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
```

  > I would like to provide some findings here. I looked at the NVIDIA DRA driver code to understand the actual need. In partitionable mode (DynamicMIG), the driver publishes consumesCounters on both whole GPUs and MIG devices and whole GPUs consume the full counter set. So the primary path (consumesCounters → actual value) covers everything without needing deviceSelector.
  
  I am not sure I understand, the driver providing consumesCounter for the whole devices is for the accounting of resources at the DRA level (between scheduler and driver).
  
  This thread is about the right UX for admins to configure the quota for whole devices as well as partitionable devices.  IMO here are the usecases that should all work:
  1. admin should be able to set up quota for whole devices (this is the alpha functionility).
  1. admin should be able to set up quota for whole device and partitionable devices i.e. 
     1.  when users use partitionable devices to request quota from whole devices, it should work the same as users requesting whole devices. Kueue should proper accounting.
  1. admin should be able to set up quota exclusively for partitionable devices
  
  for requirement (2) either there are two alternatives:
  1. the admin can define a rough estimate of how much memory each devices consumes, or
  2. the admin provides the accelerator name, that kueue can look up the memory advertised by the DRA driver
  
  I prefer option (2) but I will leave it up to other reviewers to share their thoughts. cc @kannon92 @tenzen-y @mimowo

  @alaypatel07 As discussed over the call, I went ahead and looked into the Consumable Capacity API (KEP-5075) to check how the current config shape holds up. The core difference between Partitionable Devices and Consumable Capacity is the charge source. PD reads from the device (`consumesCounters`) and CC reads from the ResourceClaimTemplate's device request (`capacity.requests`). But from Kueue's perspective, both produce the same output: a resource.Quantity that gets charged against the (Flavor, Resource) → int64 quota model. The config just needs to identify which device resource dimension maps to which Kueue quota resource, that works for both. The implementation would add a new code path that reads `capacity.requests` from the RCT instead of matching devices in ResourceSlices, but the quota tracking stays the same.

+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new `counterMappings` field in the Configuration API maps driver counters to Kueue quota
+resources. Each counter mapping binds a DeviceClass to a counter-based quota resource. A
+DeviceClass can appear in either `deviceClassMappings` or `counterMappings` but not both.
+This is validated at config load time.
+
+The `Resources` struct is extended with a `CounterMappings` field:
+
+```golang
+type Resources struct {
+    // ...existing fields...
+
+    // CounterMappings defines mappings from DRA driver counters to logical resources
+    // for partitionable device quota management.
+    // +optional
+    CounterMappings []CounterMapping `json:"counterMappings,omitempty"`
```

  Yup, I initially added that under `Notes` but moved it under `Appendix` now.

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1045** by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) — 2026-05-14T13:23:58Z
```diff
@@ -876,6 +932,367 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Processing Flow](#processing-flow-1) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // When the extended resources path resolves a DeviceClass whose mapping
```

  same as above, drop extended resources with counters, not supported

+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml

+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com

+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1

+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set

+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // When the extended resources path resolves a DeviceClass whose mapping
+    // has counters, it derives the counter charge instead of using device count.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // DeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving the whole-device
+    // counter charge from ResourceSlices. When a matched device has no
+    // consumesCounters (e.g., a whole GPU), Kueue uses the selector to find
+    // pools with matching devices and reads sharedCounters[counterName] as the
+    // charge. The selector is compiled at config load time using the upstream
+    // dracel compiler. When omitted, devices without consumesCounters are
+    // marked inadmissible.
+    // +optional
+    DeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"deviceSelector,omitempty"`
```

  resourcev1.DeviceSelector is very broad, do we need all of its functionality?
  
  Is there a way to limit the CEL expression such that it is computationally cheap?

+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // DeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving the whole-device
+    // counter charge from ResourceSlices. When a matched device has no
+    // consumesCounters (e.g., a whole GPU), Kueue uses the selector to find
+    // pools with matching devices and reads sharedCounters[counterName] as the
+    // charge. The selector is compiled at config load time using the upstream
+    // dracel compiler. When omitted, devices without consumesCounters are
+    // marked inadmissible.
+    // +optional
+    DeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"deviceSelector,omitempty"`
```

  should it rather be named `sharedCounterDeviceSelector`?

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1181** by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) — 2026-05-14T13:52:01Z
```diff
@@ -876,6 +932,367 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Processing Flow](#processing-flow-1) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // When the extended resources path resolves a DeviceClass whose mapping
+    // has counters, it derives the counter charge instead of using device count.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // DeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving the whole-device
+    // counter charge from ResourceSlices. When a matched device has no
+    // consumesCounters (e.g., a whole GPU), Kueue uses the selector to find
+    // pools with matching devices and reads sharedCounters[counterName] as the
+    // charge. The selector is compiled at config load time using the upstream
+    // dracel compiler. When omitted, devices without consumesCounters are
+    // marked inadmissible.
+    // +optional
+    DeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"deviceSelector,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup. Drivers that publish `consumesCounters` on both whole
+GPUs and MIG devices (e.g., NVIDIA with DynamicMIG enabled) do not need `deviceSelector`:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+For drivers that publish `consumesCounters` only on partitioned devices but not on whole
+devices, `deviceSelector` derives the whole-device charge from `sharedCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.example.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.example.com
+      counterName: memory
+      deviceSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.example.com'].productName == 'ExampleGPU'"
+```
+
+When a matched device has `consumesCounters`, Kueue uses the actual value. When a matched
+device has no `consumesCounters` and `deviceSelector` is configured, Kueue uses the
+selector to find pools with matching devices, reads `sharedCounters[counterName]` from
+those pools, and charges that value. When neither is available, the workload is marked
+inadmissible.
+
+When `counters` is absent on a mapping, the mapping behaves exactly as today (device-count
+quota). This is backward compatible and covers non-GPU DRA devices where device count is
+the appropriate unit.
+
+Counter names are driver-specific (NVIDIA uses `memory`, another vendor may use `gpu-mem`),
+so `counters` translates them to the parent mapping's Kueue quota resource name. Not all
+counters are quota-relevant. NVIDIA MIG devices have 15+ counter dimensions. The admin
+chooses which counters to track for quota. The scheduler handles the rest.
+
+ClusterQueue uses counter resources like any other resource:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "800Gi"   # 10 GPUs x 80Gi, in counter units
+```
+
+#### Processing Flow
+
+Partitionable devices reuses the upstream `dracel` compiler from the CEL expression
+support but performs its own
+ResourceSlice listing with pool-aware processing, since the CEL validation path does not
+return matched device objects or do pool grouping:
+
+1. Kueue looks up the RCT's DeviceClass in `deviceClassMappings`
+2. If the mapping has `counters` configured, enters the counter-based path
+3. Lists ResourceSlices for the configured driver, groups them by pool and checks completeness
+4. Compiles DeviceClass selectors (pre-filter) and RCT request selectors using the
+   `dracel` compiler, evaluates both against devices in complete pools to produce
+   matched devices
+5. For each matched device, resolves the counter charge (in order):
+   - Device has `consumesCounters` containing the configured `counterName`: uses the
+     actual value (e.g., 9856Mi for a 1g.10gb MIG profile)
+   - Device has `consumesCounters` but `counterName` is not found: workload is
+     marked inadmissible
+   - Device has no `consumesCounters` and `deviceSelector` is configured: Kueue
```

  This is not what I expressed in https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10283#discussion_r3235129534
  
  There are two distinct problems:
  1. how does admin configure quota for partitionable device?
  2. how does kueue charge the quota for a partitionable device?
  
  The KEP currently reflects deviceSelector as mechanism for resolving 2nd option. In my comment here: https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10283#discussion_r3235129534, I was suggesting using device selector for first problem.

  ```yaml
  resources:
    deviceClassMappings:
    - name: gpu.memory

### Review by [@sohankunkerkar](https://github.com/sohankunkerkar) (commented) — 2026-05-14T15:25:51Z

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:828** by [@sohankunkerkar](https://github.com/sohankunkerkar) — 2026-05-14T15:25:51Z
```diff
@@ -771,25 +821,31 @@ continues to use `deviceClassMappings`.
 
 #### Path Separation
 
-The two DRA paths have independent quota resolution:
+The two DRA paths resolve quota independently by default:
 1. **Extended resources** (`DRAExtendedResources` gate): auto-discovers DeviceClass via
-   field indexer, uses `extendedResourceName` as quota key. No `deviceClassMappings` needed.
+   field indexer. Uses `extendedResourceName` as the default quota key. If the resolved
+   DeviceClass is also present in `deviceClassMappings`, Kueue uses the mapped logical
+   name instead to unify quota with the ResourceClaimTemplate path. When the mapping has
```

  Makes sense. I initially added counter-aware extended resources to address Pannaga's [point](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10283#discussion_r3230166089) about unified charging for workloads using both nvidia.com/gpu: 1 and MIG RCTs. But you're right Extended Resources don't carry profile-level specificity, so the charge would always be the max across all matched devices. That defeats the  purpose of counter-based quota. Will revert to rejecting workloads where extended resources resolve to a DeviceClass with counters configured.

+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // When the extended resources path resolves a DeviceClass whose mapping
+    // has counters, it derives the counter charge instead of using device count.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // DeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving the whole-device
+    // counter charge from ResourceSlices. When a matched device has no
+    // consumesCounters (e.g., a whole GPU), Kueue uses the selector to find
+    // pools with matching devices and reads sharedCounters[counterName] as the
+    // charge. The selector is compiled at config load time using the upstream
+    // dracel compiler. When omitted, devices without consumesCounters are
+    // marked inadmissible.
+    // +optional
+    DeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"deviceSelector,omitempty"`
```

  We don't need the full functionality,  a simple attribute match covers the use case. The DRA CEL environment is already constrained: it only accesses `device.driver`, `device.attributes`, and `device.capacity`. Kubernetes enforces a runtime cost limit (`CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost = 1000000`) on every evaluation, and device attributes are bounded at 32 per device. Using DeviceSelector avoids defining a custom type and reuses the syntax admins already know from DeviceClass selectors. The derivation result will be cached so the evaluation doesn't repeat per workload.

 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Processing Flow](#processing-flow-1) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // When the extended resources path resolves a DeviceClass whose mapping
+    // has counters, it derives the counter charge instead of using device count.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // DeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving the whole-device
+    // counter charge from ResourceSlices. When a matched device has no
+    // consumesCounters (e.g., a whole GPU), Kueue uses the selector to find
```

  I think the the selector is for quota derivation, not per-workload charging. If multiple pools match, Kueue uses the maximum `sharedCounters[counterName]` value to derive the per-device capacity. Within a `ResourceFlavor`, GPUs are expected to be homogeneous so all values should be the same. The max is a safe default for edge cases.  Per-workload charges always come from `consumesCounters` on the matched device.

+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0

+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:

+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu

+

+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Extended resource requests that resolve to a DeviceClass with counters
+    // are marked inadmissible.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name to track for quota. Used in two places:
+    // 1. At workload admission: matches entries in device consumesCounters
+    //    to determine the per-workload charge.
+    // 2. At quota derivation: matches entries in pool sharedCounters to
+    //    determine the per-device capacity when nominalQuota is in device
+    //    units and sharedCounterDeviceSelector is configured.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // SharedCounterDeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving
+    // the counter budget from nominalQuota. When configured, Kueue uses this
+    // selector to find pools with matching devices, reads
+    // sharedCounters[counterName] to determine the per-device counter
+    // capacity, and multiplies by nominalQuota to derive the effective
+    // counter budget. The selector is compiled at config load time using
+    // the upstream dracel compiler. The DRA CEL environment is constrained
+    // to device properties only (driver, attributes, capacity) with a
+    // runtime cost limit enforced by Kubernetes. This allows the admin to set
+    // nominalQuota in device units (e.g., "10") while Kueue internally
+    // tracks counter units (e.g., 800Gi). When omitted, nominalQuota is
+    // used directly as counter units.
+    // +optional
+    SharedCounterDeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"sharedCounterDeviceSelector,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup with `sharedCounterDeviceSelector`. The admin sets
+`nominalQuota` in device units and Kueue derives the counter budget:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      sharedCounterDeviceSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].productName == 'A100-SXM4-80GB'"
+---
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "10"   # 10 GPUs; Kueue derives 10 * 80Gi = 800Gi
+```
+
+When `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` is configured, Kueue evaluates the selector against
+devices across pools for the configured driver, reads `sharedCounters[counterName]` from
+matching pools to determine the per-device counter capacity, and multiplies by
+`nominalQuota` to derive the effective counter budget. If the selector matches devices
```

  This seems very suspicious for Kueue resource model tbh, because it seems to completely ignore resource borroting and COhorts. In Kueue we have prod users operating on ClusterQueues:
  - which have `nominalQuota: 0`, but the `nominalQuota` is defined in Cohort
  - have `nominalQuota: 0` but the capacity is borrowed from other CLusterQueues

+    // used directly as counter units.
+    // +optional
+    SharedCounterDeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"sharedCounterDeviceSelector,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup with `sharedCounterDeviceSelector`. The admin sets
+`nominalQuota` in device units and Kueue derives the counter budget:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      sharedCounterDeviceSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].productName == 'A100-SXM4-80GB'"
+---
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "10"   # 10 GPUs; Kueue derives 10 * 80Gi = 800Gi
+```
+
+When `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` is configured, Kueue evaluates the selector against
+devices across pools for the configured driver, reads `sharedCounters[counterName]` from
+matching pools to determine the per-device counter capacity, and multiplies by
+`nominalQuota` to derive the effective counter budget. If the selector matches devices
```

  @alaypatel07 I think @mimowo raised a valid point about Cohorts and borrowing. The derivation model (nominalQuota * perDeviceCapacity) doesn't work when nominalQuota: 0, which is a real production pattern with Cohort-based capacity.
   
  I have a proposal for Alpha: keep `nominalQuota` in counter units for correctness with Cohorts/borrowing. The `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` can serve as a validation and discovery tool. Kueue evaluates it at config time and surfaces the per-device counter capacity in logs or status, helping the admin compute the right nominalQuota value. The admin sets nominalQuota: "800Gi" (10 * 80Gi) directly. No runtime derivation, Cohorts work unchanged.                                                                               
  
  For Beta, we can explore a kueuectl helper or ClusterQueue status field that shows the equivalent device count (e.g., "800Gi ≈ 10 A100-80GB GPUs") based on the selector, maybe?
  
  Thoughts?

### Review by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) (commented) — 2026-05-14T18:17:00Z

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1141** by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) — 2026-05-14T18:17:00Z
```diff
@@ -876,6 +931,342 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Processing Flow](#processing-flow-1) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Extended resource requests that resolve to a DeviceClass with counters
+    // are marked inadmissible.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name to track for quota. Used in two places:
+    // 1. At workload admission: matches entries in device consumesCounters
+    //    to determine the per-workload charge.
+    // 2. At quota derivation: matches entries in pool sharedCounters to
+    //    determine the per-device capacity when nominalQuota is in device
+    //    units and sharedCounterDeviceSelector is configured.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // SharedCounterDeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving
+    // the counter budget from nominalQuota. When configured, Kueue uses this
+    // selector to find pools with matching devices, reads
+    // sharedCounters[counterName] to determine the per-device counter
+    // capacity, and multiplies by nominalQuota to derive the effective
+    // counter budget. The selector is compiled at config load time using
+    // the upstream dracel compiler. The DRA CEL environment is constrained
+    // to device properties only (driver, attributes, capacity) with a
+    // runtime cost limit enforced by Kubernetes. This allows the admin to set
+    // nominalQuota in device units (e.g., "10") while Kueue internally
+    // tracks counter units (e.g., 800Gi). When omitted, nominalQuota is
+    // used directly as counter units.
+    // +optional
+    SharedCounterDeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"sharedCounterDeviceSelector,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup with `sharedCounterDeviceSelector`. The admin sets
+`nominalQuota` in device units and Kueue derives the counter budget:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      sharedCounterDeviceSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].productName == 'A100-SXM4-80GB'"
+---
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "10"   # 10 GPUs; Kueue derives 10 * 80Gi = 800Gi
+```
+
+When `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` is configured, Kueue evaluates the selector against
+devices across pools for the configured driver, reads `sharedCounters[counterName]` from
+matching pools to determine the per-device counter capacity, and multiplies by
+`nominalQuota` to derive the effective counter budget. If the selector matches devices
```

  > @alaypatel07 I think @mimowo raised a valid point about Cohorts and borrowing. The derivation model (nominalQuota * perDeviceCapacity) doesn't work when nominalQuota: 0, which is a real production pattern with Cohort-based capacity.
  
  I dont completely follow why this will be problematic. With partitionable devices, kueue allows a change in resource model which can allow users to configure along different dimensions of counters (memory, compute etc). If counters are configured, they should only be able to borrow capacity from other counters, it does not make sense to allow a counter to borrow memory (a dimension) from a pool which does not have dimensions.
  
   
  
  > I have a proposal for Alpha: keep nominalQuota in counter units for correctness with Cohorts/borrowing. The sharedCounterDeviceSelector can serve as a validation and discovery tool. Kueue evaluates it at config time and surfaces the per-device counter capacity in logs or status, helping the admin compute the right nominalQuota value. The admin sets nominalQuota: "800Gi" (10 * 80Gi) directly. No runtime derivation, Cohorts work unchanged.
  
  With this approach my concern is that each new device in the cluster will require admin to monitor and audit the quotas. I am not convinced pre-declaring static quota is a user experience kueue should be providing to admins, when DRA provides ability to partition the device on-the-fly.

  @alaypatel07 Thanks for the inputs. You're right on both points. The derivation math works with Cohorts. IIUC, @mimowo's concern is not just about implementation risk but about changing Kueue's resource model. Today the resource model is pure: nominalQuota is what the admin sets, stored directly, used directly, no transformations, no external dependencies. With derivation, the stored quota value is not what the admin configured but it's transformed based on external ResourceSlice data. The cache layer would depend on DRA runtime state to compute quota values, which is a fundamental shift from "quota is what you see" to "quota is derived from external data." That changes the model's contract, not just the code.
  
  The question is whether this model change is the right direction, or whether counter-unit nominalQuota (admin computes once, no transformation) is more aligned with how Kueue's resource model works today.

+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:

+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Extended resource requests that resolve to a DeviceClass with counters
+    // are marked inadmissible.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name to track for quota. Used in two places:
+    // 1. At workload admission: matches entries in device consumesCounters
+    //    to determine the per-workload charge.
+    // 2. At quota derivation: matches entries in pool sharedCounters to
+    //    determine the per-device capacity when nominalQuota is in device
+    //    units and sharedCounterDeviceSelector is configured.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // SharedCounterDeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving
+    // the counter budget from nominalQuota. When configured, Kueue uses this
+    // selector to find pools with matching devices, reads
+    // sharedCounters[counterName] to determine the per-device counter
+    // capacity, and multiplies by nominalQuota to derive the effective
+    // counter budget. The selector is compiled at config load time using
+    // the upstream dracel compiler. The DRA CEL environment is constrained
+    // to device properties only (driver, attributes, capacity) with a
+    // runtime cost limit enforced by Kubernetes. This allows the admin to set
+    // nominalQuota in device units (e.g., "10") while Kueue internally
+    // tracks counter units (e.g., 800Gi). When omitted, nominalQuota is
+    // used directly as counter units.
+    // +optional
+    SharedCounterDeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"sharedCounterDeviceSelector,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup with `sharedCounterDeviceSelector`. The admin sets
+`nominalQuota` in device units and Kueue derives the counter budget:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      sharedCounterDeviceSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].productName == 'A100-SXM4-80GB'"
+---
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "10"   # 10 GPUs; Kueue derives 10 * 80Gi = 800Gi
+```
+
+When `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` is configured, Kueue evaluates the selector against
+devices across pools for the configured driver, reads `sharedCounters[counterName]` from
+matching pools to determine the per-device counter capacity, and multiplies by
+`nominalQuota` to derive the effective counter budget. If the selector matches devices
```

  > Today the resource model is pure: nominalQuota is what the admin sets, stored directly, used directly, no transformations, no external dependencies. With derivation, the stored quota value is not what the admin configured but it's transformed based on external ResourceSlice data. The cache layer would depend on DRA runtime state to compute quota values, which is a fundamental shift from "quota is what you see" to "quota is derived from external data." That changes the model's contract, not just the code.
  
  I agree with this. The indirection comes with a cost. However, the resource model "quota is what you see" is fundamentally different from what partitionable devices API allows. With partitionable devices API, users defer the decision of which device gets allocated all the way till scheduling time, and that is the core advantage of feature. 
  
  So the challenge here is to find the middle ground where both the models can converge safely.
  
  If you want a statically defined quota, the correct model is to:
  1. use device plugins
  2. configure the nodes with the right shapes of the device
  3. configure the quota in terms of memory using the device class mapping
  
  If folks feel that for alpha it is okay to not find the right middle ground and we should start somewhere, its fine to me, but this thought should be captured.
