```diff
@@ -876,6 +931,342 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Processing Flow](#processing-flow-1) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Extended resource requests that resolve to a DeviceClass with counters
+    // are marked inadmissible.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name to track for quota. Used in two places:
+    // 1. At workload admission: matches entries in device consumesCounters
+    //    to determine the per-workload charge.
+    // 2. At quota derivation: matches entries in pool sharedCounters to
+    //    determine the per-device capacity when nominalQuota is in device
+    //    units and sharedCounterDeviceSelector is configured.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // SharedCounterDeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving
+    // the counter budget from nominalQuota. When configured, Kueue uses this
+    // selector to find pools with matching devices, reads
+    // sharedCounters[counterName] to determine the per-device counter
+    // capacity, and multiplies by nominalQuota to derive the effective
+    // counter budget. The selector is compiled at config load time using
+    // the upstream dracel compiler. The DRA CEL environment is constrained
+    // to device properties only (driver, attributes, capacity) with a
+    // runtime cost limit enforced by Kubernetes. This allows the admin to set
+    // nominalQuota in device units (e.g., "10") while Kueue internally
+    // tracks counter units (e.g., 800Gi). When omitted, nominalQuota is
+    // used directly as counter units.
+    // +optional
+    SharedCounterDeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"sharedCounterDeviceSelector,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup with `sharedCounterDeviceSelector`. The admin sets
+`nominalQuota` in device units and Kueue derives the counter budget:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      sharedCounterDeviceSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].productName == 'A100-SXM4-80GB'"
+---
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "10"   # 10 GPUs; Kueue derives 10 * 80Gi = 800Gi
+```
+
+When `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` is configured, Kueue evaluates the selector against
+devices across pools for the configured driver, reads `sharedCounters[counterName]` from
+matching pools to determine the per-device counter capacity, and multiplies by
+`nominalQuota` to derive the effective counter budget. If the selector matches devices
```

  >If you want a statically defined quota, the correct model is to:
  
  >use device plugins
  >configure the nodes with the right shapes of the device
  >configure the quota in terms of memory using the device class mapping
  
  I would like to clarify here: the per-workload charge is fully dynamic. When a workload arrives, Kueue reads `consumesCounters` from actual ResourceSlice data at admission time. A 1g.10gb is charged 9856Mi, a 7g.80gb is charged 80Gi and all from live driver data. The only static part is the total budget (nominalQuota: "800Gi"), which the admin sets once. This is different from the device plugin model where both the capacity and the per-workload charge are statically pre-configured.
  
  That said, I agree the static budget is a limitation. When GPUs are added or removed, the admin needs to update nominalQuota. Happy to capture this as an Alpha limitation.
  
  For Beta, one approach that avoids changing the resource model: the `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` derivation can happen in `createResourceQuotas` at parse time (the same boundary where CPU millicores conversion already happens via ResourceValue). The admin sets `nominalQuota: "10" (devices)`, Kueue multiplies by the per-device capacity from sharedCounters before storing. By the time values reach the cache layer, they're plain counter units. The cache layer, Cohorts, borrowing, hot path, all unchanged. The
  multiplication runs once per CQ reconcile, not per workload admission. This keeps the resource model intact while giving the admin device-unit UX. 
  @alaypatel07 @mimowo Thoughts?

  > I would like to clarify here: the per-workload charge is fully dynamic. When a workload arrives, Kueue reads consumesCounters from actual ResourceSlice data at admission time. A 1g.10gb is charged 9856Mi, a 7g.80gb is charged 80Gi and all from live driver data. The only static part is the total budget (nominalQuota: "800Gi"), which the admin sets once. This is different from the device plugin model where both the capacity and the per-workload charge are statically pre-configured.
  
   don’t think this is 100% accurate. At admission time, the workload may request any available partition, and its CEL selectors may match more than one device shape.
  
  For example, suppose the matching devices have:
  - dev-a: consumesCounters.memory = 10Gi
  - dev-b: consumesCounters.memory = 20Gi
  - dev-c: consumesCounters.memory = 40Gi
  
  The NVIDIA MIG DeviceClass is an example of this pattern: it selects MIG-capable devices, but does not necessarily select one exact profile:
  https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/blob/3687fd54d337b35f37ade08389cabdc380b734a9/deployments/helm/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/templates/deviceclass-mig.yaml#L10
  
  In that case, Kueue does not know which device kube-scheduler will allocate. The conservative admission-time charge would be:
  ```
  per-workload charge = max(consumesCounters[counterName]) across all matching devices
  ```
  
  That avoids undercharging, but it means Kueue may overcharge quota.
  
  This is why I think a selector in the Kueue counter configuration is useful. It lets the admin define a homogeneous accounting domain, for example “this quota is for A100 1g.10gb-shaped partitions,” where all matching devices are expected to have the same counter consumption. If the workload request is not compatible with that accounting domain, Kueue can reject it or fall back to conservative max charging.
  
  If adding the selector is too disruptive for Alpha, then we can keep both sides static: the admin configures the total counter capacity and the default/maximum per-workload charge. But that takes me back to my original concern: both the capacity declaration and the quota accounting become admin-maintained approximations as expressed here: https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10283/changes#r3243697874

```diff
@@ -876,6 +931,342 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Processing Flow](#processing-flow-1) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Extended resource requests that resolve to a DeviceClass with counters
+    // are marked inadmissible.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name to track for quota. Used in two places:
+    // 1. At workload admission: matches entries in device consumesCounters
+    //    to determine the per-workload charge.
+    // 2. At quota derivation: matches entries in pool sharedCounters to
+    //    determine the per-device capacity when nominalQuota is in device
+    //    units and sharedCounterDeviceSelector is configured.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // SharedCounterDeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving
+    // the counter budget from nominalQuota. When configured, Kueue uses this
+    // selector to find pools with matching devices, reads
+    // sharedCounters[counterName] to determine the per-device counter
+    // capacity, and multiplies by nominalQuota to derive the effective
+    // counter budget. The selector is compiled at config load time using
+    // the upstream dracel compiler. The DRA CEL environment is constrained
+    // to device properties only (driver, attributes, capacity) with a
+    // runtime cost limit enforced by Kubernetes. This allows the admin to set
+    // nominalQuota in device units (e.g., "10") while Kueue internally
+    // tracks counter units (e.g., 800Gi). When omitted, nominalQuota is
+    // used directly as counter units.
+    // +optional
+    SharedCounterDeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"sharedCounterDeviceSelector,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup with `sharedCounterDeviceSelector`. The admin sets
+`nominalQuota` in device units and Kueue derives the counter budget:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      sharedCounterDeviceSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].productName == 'A100-SXM4-80GB'"
+---
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "10"   # 10 GPUs; Kueue derives 10 * 80Gi = 800Gi
+```
+
+When `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` is configured, Kueue evaluates the selector against
+devices across pools for the configured driver, reads `sharedCounters[counterName]` from
+matching pools to determine the per-device counter capacity, and multiplies by
+`nominalQuota` to derive the effective counter budget. If the selector matches devices
```

  It sounds like we have three possible directions. Let me try to summarize them so we can decide explicitly.
  
  ### Option 1: Counter-unit quota only for Alpha
  
  Admins configure quota directly in counter units:
  
  ```yaml
  resources:
  - name: gpu.memory
    nominalQuota: "800Gi"
  ```
  
  Kueue charges workloads using consumesCounters from ResourceSlices. For broad selectors that match multiple device shapes, Kueue charges conservatively, for example max(consumesCounters.memory) across matching devices.
  
  Pros:
  
  - Fits Kueue’s current aggregate (Flavor, Resource) -> Quantity model.
  - Cohorts, borrowing, lending, and preemption work unchanged.
  - Smallest implementation surface for Alpha.
  
  Cons:
  
  - Admins must compute and maintain the aggregate counter budget manually.
  - GPU capacity changes require quota updates.
  - Broad selectors may lead to overcharging.
  - Does not give admins a device-count UX for partitionable devices.
  
  ### Option 2: Counter-unit quota plus config selector for homogeneous accounting domains
  
  ```yaml
  resources:
    deviceClassMappings:
    - name: gpu.memory
      deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
      counters:
      - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
        counterName: memory
        selector:
          cel:
            expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
  ---
  resources:
  - name: gpu.memory
    nominalQuota: "800GiB"
  ```
  
  Pros:
  
  - Still preserves Kueue’s current quota model.
  - Lets admins define homogeneous device/counter domains.
  - Avoids ambiguous charging when broad DeviceClasses match multiple profiles.
  - Can reject incompatible workloads or fall back to max charging.
  
  Cons: 
  - Adds CEL config surface and validation/evaluation work.
  - Workload selectors and config selectors need clear compatibility semantics.
  - Admins still configure total counter quota manually.
  
  ### Option 3: Device-unit UX with derived effective counter quota
  Admins configure quota in device units, and Kueue derives the effective counter quota from ResourceSlices using a selector:
  ```yaml
  counters:
  - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
    counterName: memory
    sharedCounterDeviceSelector:
      cel:
        expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].productName == 'A100'"
  ---
  resources:
  - name: gpu.memory
    nominalQuota: "10" # device units; Kueue derives effective counter quota
  ```
  Pros:
  
  - Best admin UX when admins think in “number of GPUs.”
  - Avoids duplicating GPU memory sizes in Kueue config.
  - Uses ResourceSlices as the source of truth for counter capacity.
  
  Cons:
  - Changes/extends the quota model because configured quota and effective quota have different units.
  - Needs explicit semantics for ClusterQueue and Cohort resourceGroups.
  - Must apply consistently to nominalQuota, borrowingLimit, and lendingLimit.
  - Needs status/debuggability for configured vs effective quota.
  - Needs recomputation when relevant ResourceSlices change.
  - Probably too large for Alpha.
  
  
  My preference order is: option 2, option 1, option 3.  But I am okay if option 1 is preferable as long as the pros and cons are agreed upon and documented.

  I think we should use Option 1 for alpha because it preserves Kueue’s resource model and gives us a correct, composable base API. Then we can add higher-level UX improvements later, either through tooling/status or a carefully designed selector-based enhancement, without committing alpha to quota derivation semantics that are still unsettled.

+    //    determine the per-device capacity when nominalQuota is in device
+    //    units and sharedCounterDeviceSelector is configured.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // SharedCounterDeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving
+    // the counter budget from nominalQuota. When configured, Kueue uses this
+    // selector to find pools with matching devices, reads
+    // sharedCounters[counterName] to determine the per-device counter
+    // capacity, and multiplies by nominalQuota to derive the effective
+    // counter budget. The selector is compiled at config load time using
+    // the upstream dracel compiler. The DRA CEL environment is constrained
+    // to device properties only (driver, attributes, capacity) with a
+    // runtime cost limit enforced by Kubernetes. This allows the admin to set
+    // nominalQuota in device units (e.g., "10") while Kueue internally
+    // tracks counter units (e.g., 800Gi). When omitted, nominalQuota is
+    // used directly as counter units.
+    // +optional
+    SharedCounterDeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"sharedCounterDeviceSelector,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup with `sharedCounterDeviceSelector`. The admin sets
+`nominalQuota` in device units and Kueue derives the counter budget:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      sharedCounterDeviceSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].productName == 'A100-SXM4-80GB'"
+---
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "10"   # 10 GPUs; Kueue derives 10 * 80Gi = 800Gi
+```
+
+When `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` is configured, Kueue evaluates the selector against
+devices across pools for the configured driver, reads `sharedCounters[counterName]` from
+matching pools to determine the per-device counter capacity, and multiplies by
+`nominalQuota` to derive the effective counter budget. If the selector matches devices
```

  To clarify my earlier comment, I meant Option 1's quota model (counter-unit nominalQuota) as the base, which Option 2 also uses and adds a selector for accurate per-workload charging on top of the same quota model. Since the selector lives in the DRA processing path and doesn't touch the quota model or cache layer, I'm open to including it in Alpha alongside counter-unit nominalQuota. This gives unified pool + accurate charging.

### Review by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) (commented) — 2026-05-15T15:20:03Z

- **keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1141** by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) — 2026-05-15T15:20:03Z
```diff
@@ -876,6 +931,342 @@ is treated as a normal (non-DRA) extended resource. A subsequent reconciliation
 DeviceClass is created. Event-driven re-reconciliation on DeviceClass creation will be
 evaluated as a Beta graduation criterion.
 
+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Processing Flow](#processing-flow-1) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This enables both whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool, with quota expressed in the counter's natural units.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Extended resource requests that resolve to a DeviceClass with counters
+    // are marked inadmissible.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name to track for quota. Used in two places:
+    // 1. At workload admission: matches entries in device consumesCounters
+    //    to determine the per-workload charge.
+    // 2. At quota derivation: matches entries in pool sharedCounters to
+    //    determine the per-device capacity when nominalQuota is in device
+    //    units and sharedCounterDeviceSelector is configured.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // SharedCounterDeviceSelector identifies the device shape for deriving
+    // the counter budget from nominalQuota. When configured, Kueue uses this
+    // selector to find pools with matching devices, reads
+    // sharedCounters[counterName] to determine the per-device counter
+    // capacity, and multiplies by nominalQuota to derive the effective
+    // counter budget. The selector is compiled at config load time using
+    // the upstream dracel compiler. The DRA CEL environment is constrained
+    // to device properties only (driver, attributes, capacity) with a
+    // runtime cost limit enforced by Kubernetes. This allows the admin to set
+    // nominalQuota in device units (e.g., "10") while Kueue internally
+    // tracks counter units (e.g., 800Gi). When omitted, nominalQuota is
+    // used directly as counter units.
+    // +optional
+    SharedCounterDeviceSelector *resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"sharedCounterDeviceSelector,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+```
+
+Unified whole-GPU + MIG setup with `sharedCounterDeviceSelector`. The admin sets
+`nominalQuota` in device units and Kueue derives the counter budget:
+
+```yaml
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [gpu-whole, mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      sharedCounterDeviceSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].productName == 'A100-SXM4-80GB'"
+---
+apiVersion: kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: ClusterQueue
+metadata:
+  name: gpu-queue
+spec:
+  resourceGroups:
+  - coveredResources: ["gpu.memory"]
+    flavors:
+    - name: a100-pool
+      resources:
+      - name: gpu.memory
+        nominalQuota: "10"   # 10 GPUs; Kueue derives 10 * 80Gi = 800Gi
+```
+
+When `sharedCounterDeviceSelector` is configured, Kueue evaluates the selector against
+devices across pools for the configured driver, reads `sharedCounters[counterName]` from
+matching pools to determine the per-device counter capacity, and multiplies by
+`nominalQuota` to derive the effective counter budget. If the selector matches devices
```

  > Since the selector lives in the DRA processing path and doesn't touch the quota model or cache layer, I'm open to including it in Alpha alongside counter-unit nominalQuota.
  
  +1, This is the primary reason for my preference of option 2. Will let others chime in on the API, but lgtm from my side with this.

### Review by [@alaypatel07](https://github.com/alaypatel07) (commented) — 2026-05-15T15:56:20Z

Added a couple of questions inline, but those are not blockers.

After the design reviews:
1. The API has been changed to allow admins to manage partitionable devices alongside whole devices.
2. The quota charging mechanism has been changed from being implementation logic to an API field configurable by admins allowing for narrower/stricter quota management.

Considering the above changes, LGTM. Thanks @sohankunkerkar !!

/lgtm

+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources
+with the Alpha limitations described below. Broad CEL selectors may overcharge quota
+in Alpha. See [Processing Flow](#processing-flow-1) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This can allow whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool. See [Path Interactions](#path-interactions) for caveats on unified pool charging.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Extended resource requests that resolve to a DeviceClass with counters
+    // are marked inadmissible.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // Selector narrows which devices determine the per-workload counter
+    // charge. When configured, only devices matching this selector are
+    // considered for reading consumesCounters. If a workload's matched
+    // devices include devices outside the selector scope, the workload is
+    // rejected to prevent undercharging. Accurate charging depends on the
+    // selected devices having the same counter value. The selector is
+    // compiled at config load time using the upstream dracel compiler.
+    // The DRA CEL environment is constrained
+    // to device properties only (driver, attributes, capacity) with a
```

  Keeping it optional covers two cases: single-profile DeviceClasses where all matched devices already have the same counter value, and broad DeviceClasses where the admin accepts conservative max-charging. Making it required would add config overhead for the simple case with no accuracy benefit.

  Discussed this offline. I think it make sense to keep it required as Single profile DeviceClasses are essentially whole-devices.

+### Partitionable Devices
+
+This section is gated behind the `DRAPartitionableDevices` Kueue feature gate.
+
+Kueue supports counter-based quota for partitionable DRA devices as defined by
+Kubernetes [KEP-4815](https://github.com/kubernetes/enhancements/issues/4815). Instead of
+counting devices, Kueue tracks counter consumption (e.g., GPU memory) from the
+`SharedCounters` and `ConsumesCounters` fields on ResourceSlices.
+
+Counter-based resources fit into Kueue's existing (Flavor, Resource) quota model.
+Borrowing, lending, cohorts, preemption, and fair sharing work with counter resources.
+The `consumesCounterSelector` ensures accurate charging by narrowing the accounting
+domain. See [Processing Flow](#processing-flow-1) for details.
+
+#### ResourceSlice Structure
+
+Starting in K8s 1.35, `SharedCounters` and `Devices` are mutually exclusive in a single
+ResourceSlice (`+zeroOrOneOf=ResourceSliceType`). Drivers must split them into separate
+slices in the same pool. On K8s 1.34 (where partitionable devices are alpha) this
+validation does not apply and some drivers put both in one slice
+(`resourceSliceCount: 1`). Pool completeness checks `len(slices) == resourceSliceCount`
+so both layouts work.
+
+The example below shows the separate-slice layout. Only the `memory` counter is shown.
+The driver also
+publishes `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-0` through `memory-slice-7` which the
+kube-scheduler uses for MIG placement but Kueue does not need for quota:
+
+```yaml
+# ResourceSlice 1: SharedCounters (total capacity for this GPU)
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  sharedCounters:
+  - name: gpu-0-counter-set
+    counters:
+      memory:
+        value: 80Gi
+      # Driver also publishes multiprocessors, memory-slice-0 through
+      # memory-slice-7 - used by kube-scheduler, not by Kueue.
+---
+# ResourceSlice 2: Devices with ConsumesCounters
+spec:
+  driver: gpu.nvidia.com
+  pool:
+    name: node1-gpu0
+    generation: 1
+    resourceSliceCount: 2
+  nodeName: node1
+  devices:
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-1g.10gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "1g.10gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 9856Mi
+  - name: gpu-0-mig-7g.80gb-0
+    attributes:
+      gpu.nvidia.com/profile:
+        string: "7g.80gb"
+    consumesCounters:
+    - counterSet: gpu-0-counter-set
+      counters:
+        memory:
+          value: 80Gi
+```
+
+#### User Workload
+
+Users request MIG profiles via CEL selectors on ResourceClaimTemplates. Counter tracking
+is transparent to the user:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: resource.k8s.io/v1
+kind: ResourceClaimTemplate
+metadata:
+  name: mig-1g-10gb
+spec:
+  spec:
+    devices:
+      requests:
+      - name: gpu
+        exactly:
+          deviceClassName: mig.nvidia.com
+          selectors:
+          - cel:
+              expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
+```
+
+#### Configuration
+
+The existing `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended with an optional `counters` field.
+When `counters` is present, Kueue tracks quota in counter units (e.g., GPU memory) instead
+of device count. This can allow whole-GPU and MIG DeviceClasses to share a single quota
+pool. See [Path Interactions](#path-interactions) for caveats on unified pool charging.
+
+The `DeviceClassMapping` struct is extended:
+
+```golang
+type DeviceClassMapping struct {
+    // ...existing fields (Name, DeviceClassNames)...
+
+    // Counters configures counter-based quota for partitionable devices.
+    // When present, quota is tracked in counter units instead of device count.
+    // Extended resource requests that resolve to a DeviceClass with counters
+    // are marked inadmissible.
+    // Requires the DRAPartitionableDevices feature gate.
+    // +optional
+    Counters []CounterConfig `json:"counters,omitempty"`
+}
+
+// CounterConfig maps a DRA driver counter to the parent DeviceClassMapping's
+// Kueue quota resource.
+type CounterConfig struct {
+    // DriverName is the DRA driver name used to filter relevant ResourceSlices.
+    // Must match the spec.driver field on ResourceSlice objects.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=253
+    DriverName string `json:"driverName"`
+
+    // CounterName is the counter name within the device's consumesCounters
+    // entries to track for quota. Must match a counter name published by
+    // the driver in ResourceSlice devices' consumesCounters field.
+    // Counter set names are per-GPU identifiers (e.g., gpu-0-counter-set,
+    // gpu-1-counter-set), so counterName matches across all counter sets
+    // for a given driver without requiring one mapping per GPU.
+    // +required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Required
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:MaxLength=63
+    CounterName string `json:"counterName"`
+
+    // ConsumesCounterSelector narrows which devices determine the
+    // per-workload counter charge. Only devices matching this selector are
+    // considered for reading consumesCounters. If a workload's matched
+    // devices include devices outside the selector scope, the workload is
+    // marked inadmissible to prevent undercharging. Accurate charging depends on the
+    // selected devices having the same counter value. The selector is
+    // compiled at config load time using the upstream dracel compiler.
+    // +required
+    ConsumesCounterSelector resourcev1.DeviceSelector `json:"consumesCounterSelector"`
+}
+```
+
+MIG-only setup where all matched devices have `consumesCounters`:
+
+```yaml
+apiVersion: config.kueue.x-k8s.io/v1beta2
+kind: Configuration
+featureGates:
+  DynamicResourceAllocation: true
+  DRAPartitionableDevices: true
+resources:
+  deviceClassMappings:
+  - name: gpu.memory
+    deviceClassNames: [mig.nvidia.com]
+    counters:
+    - driverName: gpu.nvidia.com
+      counterName: memory
+      consumesCounterSelector:
+        cel:
+          expression: "device.attributes['gpu.nvidia.com'].profile == '1g.10gb'"
```

  I’m having a bit of trouble following how this scales beyond a single profile, or beyond a single resource.
  
  In the example, the Kueue resource name is generic (`gpu.memory`), but the `consumesCounterSelector` is profile-specific (`profile == '1g.10gb'`). Because of that, it is not clear to me whether this configuration is intended to support only one MIG profile, or whether `1g.10gb` was chosen only for brevity.
  
  For this KEP, I think it is important to include an example that demonstrates the common multi-profile case: a single GPU memory quota pool shared by workloads requesting different profiles, for example `1g.10gb` and another larger profile (say `2g.10gb`). In particular, it would be useful to see the ConfigMap, ClusterQueue, and workloads together, and how the resulting usage is aggregated against the same `gpu.memory` quota.
  
  If the examples are too large for Design Details, I’d be fine with moving the larger examples to the Appendix.

```

  The KEP mentions that drivers may publish additional counters such as `multiprocessors` and `memory-slice-*`, while the alpha validation section says that `counters` must have exactly one entry.
  
  Could we clarify whether this is only an alpha scoping / implementation-complexity limitation, or whether there is a more fundamental design limitation that would require expanding the API?
  
  If it is the latter, then I think we should reason a bit more about the future API shape now, so that the alpha API does not require a breaking change later.
